## Tribal warrior

Tribal warrior, or savage warrior, was a broad term for a type of fighter or warrior that lived beyond "civilization", in societies that were considered "primitive" by some Faerûnian perspectives, the unifying factors being low technological progress and a lack of agriculture. For example, it would be extremely unlikely to encounter such warriors in the Dalelands or on the Rock of Bral. The term could also refer to warriors who originated from such cultures and maintained a connection to them whilst living in another land or culture.

Culture
Savage warriors were considered by some to be comparatively more technologically "primitive" than barbarians or berserkers, more distanced from civilization, and more in tune with nature. This perceived "primitivism" of the savage warrior's tribal culture was not reflective of its moral, ethical, or alignment values. A "savage" tribe could easily be a peaceful people with a highly developed culture and sense of artistry.

The regions and cultures in the Realms where one could encounter those labeled as "savage warriors" included Evermeet among the most savage members of the green elves, some of the Chultans living within the wild jungles of Chult, some of the people of the Savage Frontier like the Uthgardt, and the Mazticans of Maztica.

While some warriors could be found in tribal societies, a savage warrior could also be someone who was raised by animals in the wilderness. The tribes that hosted savage warriors varied considerably, having in common only that they were less technologically advanced than other cultures in the Realms and generally did not engage in agriculture, instead subsisting upon foraging, fishing, and hunting. While some may not have taken to metallurgy, other societies could be users of bronze or iron. Some societies may have members that acted in accordance with the wishes of a ruling chief, who was either their greatest or oldest warrior or an individual who had been blessed by gods. Others could live along rivers, valuing the use of canoes, or be nomadic and valuing horseriding.

A savage warrior could be of nearly any sentient race, including humans and the various demihuman races. This type of warrior was particularly common among neanderthals.

Abilities
They were highly versatile among the various types of warriors in the Realms, as survival in the wilderness necessitated being skilled in a wide range of disciplines. These skills usually fell within the purview of the arts of fishing, forestry, hunting, and trapping. Additionally, many were able to perform a spell-like ability, most commonly resembling either alarm, animal friendship, detect evil, or detect magic. This ability was not truly a form of magic and detect magic effects would not register it as such.

With the exception of detect evil, these abilities differed subtly from the spells they resembled. Alarm would merely alert the warrior of activity, not set off an actual alarm. Animal friendship required the warrior to get up close and personal with their target and needed two castings if an animal was angry with, or threatened by, the warrior. And detect magic could not determine the school of magic of detected magics.

Combat
The weaponry typically found in the hands of a savage warrior included the likes of blowguns (mainly in jungle environs), clubs, daggers, javelins, knives, longbows, shortbows, slings, spears, handaxes, throwing axes, and quarterstaves. Depending on the culture a warrior came from their weapons could be made out of bone, stone, bronze, or iron. In terms of clothing and armor, they often garbed themselves in what was common among the culture they came from, such as hide armor. The garments of "civilized" societies were perceived to be cumbersome and uncomfortable to savage warriors, negatively impacting their ability to concentrate in the heat of battle.

Notable Tribal Warriors
 Gryhark, an Elk tribe Uthgardt.
 Kixitiloc, a Maztican adventurer of the Payit ethnicity.
 Mend-nets, a member of the Ice Hunters tribe that made their home on Oyaviggaton during the late 15 century DR.
 Rahl Zuberi, a reptile trainer at Fort Beluarian in the late 15 century DR.

Appendix
See Also
 Savage druid
 Savage priest
 Savage wizard

Background
The "Savage" archetype of class kits introduced in AD&D with the PHBR series of accessories, starting with The Complete Fighter's Handbook, are rooted in the Noble Savage stock character of Western anthropology and literature and to some extent the Wild Man archetype. The Complete Fighter's Handbook makes this evident with its multiple uses of the term "noble" and one of its examples of a "savage" character: "... a beautiful native princess from a culture which the characters consider impossibly primitive and yet uncorrupted and very noble..." The text even suggests that players coming "from a particularly noble tribe" could choose to act as a "voice of conscience", critiquing the seeming civilized practices of other players, much as Noble Savage characters were used to critique contemporary European societies.

It should also be noted that 'primitive' originally meant 'first', 'earliest', or 'original', while 'savage' meant 'untamed' or 'wild' in the sense of wilderness and living in nature rather than cities, and have been misapplied to mean 'crude' or 'violent', but the earlier definitions are still in use. Hence, the former meanings are more accurate to the case of primitive or savage characters: peoples living in the wilderness with the earliest tools and societies, as reflected above.

## Tranquility Grove

Tranquility Grove was a mushroom-filled cavern near the drow city of Sschindylryn, and a peril to unwary travelers.

Description
The Grove was a loop of cavern filled with mushrooms, some of which were bioluminescent. What made it particularly notable was the soporific quality of the mushroom spores, which relaxed and calmed those that breathed it in. If someone fell asleep in Tranquility Grove, they were unlikely to ever wake and simply eventually die in their sleep. It was said that their souls would remain trapped in the Grove, tormented and unable to reach their final rest.

Fortunately for the local drow, elves were immune to the effects of the spores.

Geography
The cavern was located not far from the Great Waterfall and the drow spider hatchery.

Inhabitants

The cavern was home to few except the unfortunates who wandered in and fell into an eternal slumber, such as yuan-ti expeditionaries, illithids, and the drow that came to laugh at them. A number of will-o'-wisps also floated about, believed to be the unhappy souls of those who died there.

## Traitor Picks

Traitor Picks was a tavern in the Central Port of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Traitor Picks was a one-story building in poor condition.

Interior
The tavern was noted for its disreputable atmosphere and was typically overcrowded.
Services
Traitor Picks was regarded as one of Skullport's grimiest grog-and-grub establishments. Its food wass of poor quality and offered at above-average prices.

Inhabitants
Traitor Picks was owned by Denver Gilliam, who also operated the Worm's Gullet.

Traitor Picks experienced a brief period of notoriety when Gilliam recruited a well-regarded gourmet chef from Waterdeep. The chef, Percival Gallard Woodehouse, was hired to elevate the tavern’s culinary reputation. However, following an unscheduled expedition into the Underdark in the company of the notorious Volothamp Geddarm, Woodehouse returned to discover that his employment had been terminated. Gilliam had voided his contract and liquidated his possessions to cover the cost of hiring a replacement cook.

Following Woodehouse's departure, Traitor Picks has seen a rapid turnover of kitchen staff, having employed at least five different cooks in succession. In response to this instability, Gilliam has since taken a more active role in managing the establishment.

## Traegar Vadamore

Traegar Vadamore was a human Cormyrean Wizard of War during the mid-1480s DR.

Description
Traegar had light skin and short reddish hair.

Personality

Traegar was a skilled strategist and planner and came off as rather grand and verbose, even compared to the other War Wizards.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Traegar was deployed to Eveningstar to aid the war effort there. He focused on a way to prevent House Dun'Robar from staying in the war, as they provided the bulk of the soldiers and weapons used by drow armies. To this end, he sent in a team armed with rust monster ichor to infiltrate with illusions and sabotage their weapon stockpiles. Along the way, the team also faced and defeated High Battlemaster Teblyn Dun'Robar, whose favorite weapons had mysteriously fallen apart. This two-part defeat was enough to make them the laughing stock of the Underdark, and they immediately cast their suspicions at their so-called allies Szind and Avithoul.

## Trinculo

Trinculo was a mercury dragon pledged to Avachel in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Trinculo's elf guise was a Trunalor, a wild elf, with a shock of white hair, most often dressed in a purple and green robe.

Personality
The dragon was notable for his great zest and cheer.

Activities
Trinculo had taken the pledge of Avachel.

Relationships
He was good friends with Chalintash, a copper dragon, and with Khalt Laathine, a Trunalor of the Forest of Amtar.

History
Trinculo had been born in Evermeet. He had at one point met with Queen Amlaruil on the edge of the Lake of Dreams.

At some point decades before, the restless dragon had taken the pledge of Avachel to give himself something to do away from Evermeet. He'd since come to live in the Forest of Amtar, though his absences stretched for decades at a time. He'd come to be a good friend to the Trunalor there.

During the Rage of Dragons of the, Trinculo warned the elves at the Forest of Amtar to be on the lookout for dragons affected by the Rage; his friend Khalt decided to go with him, to assist him as needed, as part of his own Pledge of Avachel. Khalt accompanied him to Beregost, where Trinculo was meeting with Chalintash at the Jovial Juggler inn. In the meeting, Trinculo learned that the Rage was caused by elven High Magic; he decided he'd go to Evermeet, where together with Khalt, they might find some aid in mitigating or halting it.

Overnight, Chalintash was driven into a mindless berserker by the Rage. Trinculo and Khalt fought him together, finally executing him bitterly. While heading for Evermeet afterwards, Trinculo was overwhelmed by bitterness and grief, and gave hints he openly blamed Khalt's people for the Rage. Believing he was going to be killed, Khalt cut the ropes on his saddle, and fell into the water, not to be seen again. Trinculo regretted it soon after, and searched the water, but proved unable to find him. Finally, he continued on his way to Evermeet.

Despite descending on Evermeet possessed by the Rage, Trinculo survived until at least .

## Trader (Shining South)

A trader, as understood in the Shining South, was both a crafty merchant and a thief. Few ever bested a trader in a bargain.

Activities
These traders were often more than mere storekeepers. While some were competent shop owners who never left their homes, most were world-traveling caravan masters or master traders who represented their chaka, the core social unit of the Shining Lands, in distant regions. Thus, Durpari merchants encountered abroad were most often these traders.

Culture
Durparian traders typically worked within the law, plying their trade while adventuring. More rarely, a trader might remain at home, never leaving it for the rest of their life. Traders found it difficult to resist a bargain. A successful trader preferred to purchase something at a low price instead of just stealing it, yet they were not above listening in or sneaking about to gather the information needed to strike the best possible deal.

While trader rogues were proficient with all manner of thief weapons, they preferred smaller arms such as daggers or throwing knives that could be concealed beneath a shop counter. The light crossbow was their favored weapon, valued for how easily it could be stored in the accessible spaces of a shop or caravan wagon. Traders rarely relied on big or flashy weapons.

Traders had little use for the skills often associated with thieves. Many traders did not even own a set of lockpicks or devices like climbing boots or grappling arrows. Traders usually carried something to sell or buy and often already started their adventuring with more gold than other adventurers.

Abilities
Traders had to have at least slightly above average intelligence. These merchants were always experts at appraising an item's worth, and knowing exactly how much profit could be made. In addition to appraising common items, many traders specialized themselves in appraising certain types of more unique items like jewels or spices.

A traders' life very often encouraged them to also be proficient in information gathering, observation, linguistics, forgery (used to recognize false documents and counterfeit currency), and alertness skills.

They were also proficient in all weapons that regular thieves were also proficient in.

History
This type of trader tradition was well established by the mid–14 century DR.

Notable Traders
 Bilaska Semenir, a human master trader that encountered a group of elves whose spelljamming ship had crashed. She could not speak their language, nor could they speak hers, but within a week, she had traded them supplies, weapons, food, and whatever else they needed in return for gems that were completely unknown in the Realms.
 Lata Hisiir, a half-elf trader. She was an expert in all forms of gems and jewels.

Appendix
See Also

 Merchant-rogue, business people in Zakhara who also stole from their clients from time to time
 Smuggler, a variety of rogues that specialized in the trafficking of goods
 Swindler, traveling con artists and thieves
 Trader, Azuposi rogues who earned a living traveling and trading wares with other communities

## Tragedian

The Tragedian was an immensely valuable, elegant, enchanted mask created by a master maskcrafter alongside its sister mask–the Comedian, sometime before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The Tragedian was a powerful mask that significantly enhanced the wearer's oratory skills, such as diplomacy, intimidation, and taunting their enemies. Additionally, the mask allowed the wearer to cast a powerful version of the crushing despair spell.

History
The Tragedian and the Comedian masks were the creation of an unnamed master maskcrafter, during the time he joined a troupe of actors whilst traveling the land. To his dismay, the land was dangerous, and the troupe was in constant danger of being attacked by bandits and brigands, forcing them to take arms in self-defense. The master maskcrafter was a willful man, and he worked day and night, pouring all of his skills and magical prowess into a pair of enchanted masks. The masks were not just beautiful but also possessed great power. One night, the actors were captured by a local warlord to perform for the bandit king as much as the warlord pleased. The troupe's male lead adorned his face with the Tragedian and swayed the bandits with his honeyed words only to plunge them into a fit of cripping sadness. Then, the female lead used her mask, the Comedian, and the actors attacked the bandits, getting away from the kidnappers with enough of the stronghold's gold to consider it their payment. The bandits were left beaten and bruised, wondering if they had just watched the best play of their lives, or the worst.

By the, the Tragedian found its way into the hands of Magda, the proprietress of Mulsantir's Veil Theater.
